**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops

    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

void s343_opt(int iterations, float *flat_2d_array, float aa[256][256], float bb[256][256]) {
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 10 * (iterations / 256); nl++) {
        int k = -1;
        
        // Process each column independently
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            // Temporary arrays for this column
            float temp_vals[256];
            unsigned char mask[256];
            
            // First loop: compute mask and store values (fully vectorizable)
            // Split into two separate loops for better vectorization
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                mask[j] = (bb[j][i] > (float)0.);
            }
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                temp_vals[j] = aa[j][i];
            }
            
            // Compute prefix sum of mask using unrolled approach
            int prefix_sum[256];
            int count = 0;
            // Unroll prefix sum computation to reduce loop overhead
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j += 4) {
                prefix_sum[j] = count;
                count += mask[j];
                prefix_sum[j+1] = count;
                count += mask[j+1];
                prefix_sum[j+2] = count;
                count += mask[j+2];
                prefix_sum[j+3] = count;
                count += mask[j+3];
            }
            
            // Second loop: write using precomputed offsets (vectorizable)
            // Remove branching by using mask as condition
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                int offset = k + 1 + prefix_sum[j];
                // Use mask to conditionally write without branch
                if (mask[j]) {
                    flat_2d_array[offset] = temp_vals[j];
                }
            }
            
            // Update global k
            k += count;
        }
    }
}

In the original code, when we find a `true` condition at position `(j,i)`, we immediately write to the current `k+1` position. The optimized code tries to precompute positions but has an off-by-one error in the indexing logic.

However, the issue is more subtle: the `prefix_sum` array in the optimized code counts the number of `true` values **before** index `j`, but the write should happen at the current count. The original code does `k++` then writes to position `k`, which means the first write in a column goes to position `(previous_k + 1)`. The optimized code's `prefix_sum[j]` should be the count of `true` values **up to and including** index `j-1`, not just before `j`.

- Second true in column: `offset = k + 1 + 1 = k + 2` ✓

Optimized code:
```c

void s343_opt(int iterations, float *flat_2d_array, float aa[256][256], float bb[256][256]) {
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 10 * (iterations / 256); nl++) {
        int k = -1;
        
        // Process each column independently
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            // Temporary arrays for this column
            float temp_vals[256];
            unsigned char mask[256];
            
            // First loop: compute mask and store values (fully vectorizable)
            // Split into two separate loops for better vectorization
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                mask[j] = (bb[j][i] > (float)0.);
            }
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                temp_vals[j] = aa[j][i];
            }
            
            // Compute prefix sum of mask using unrolled approach
            int prefix_sum[256];
            int count = 0;
            // Unroll prefix sum computation to reduce loop overhead
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j += 4) {
                prefix_sum[j] = count;
                count += mask[j];
                prefix_sum[j+1] = count;
                count += mask[j+1];
                prefix_sum[j+2] = count;
                count += mask[j+2];
                prefix_sum[j+3] = count;
                count += mask[j+3];
            }
            
            // Second loop: write using precomputed offsets (vectorizable)
            // Remove branching by using mask as condition
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                int offset = k + 1 + prefix_sum[j];
                // Use mask to conditionally write without branch
                if (mask[j]) {
                    flat_2d_array[offset] = temp_vals[j];
                }
            }
            
            // Update global k
            k += count;
        }
    }
}

```

UNIT TEST PASS

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

If the access pattern involves using a[i-1] and updating a[i] within the same iteration, a dependency exists. Attempt to reorder the instructions to resolve this dependency.

The optimized code attempts to transform the original loop by:

- However, there is a **semantic error**:

  In the optimized code, the `prefix_sum` array is computed for each column independently, but `flat_2d_array` indices are computed as `k + 1 + prefix_sum[j]`, where `k` is the cumulative count from previous columns. This is correct in principle.  

But the **unit test passed**, meaning for the given test inputs, the output matched. However, there is still a subtle issue:  
The original code processes rows `j` in order for each column `i`, and writes to `flat_2d_array` in that same order. However, the original loop already had a conditional scatter that is hard to vectorize due to the unpredictable write index `k`. void s343(int iterations,float * flat_2d_array, float aa[256][256], float bb[256][256])
{

Optimized code:
```c

void s343_opt(int iterations, float *flat_2d_array, float aa[256][256], float bb[256][256]) {
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 10 * (iterations / 256); nl++) {
        int k = -1;
        
        // Process each column independently
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            // Temporary arrays for this column
            float temp_vals[256];
            unsigned char mask[256];
            
            // Loop distribution: separate mask computation and value loading
            // First compute all masks (vectorizable comparison)
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                mask[j] = (bb[j][i] > (float)0.);
            }
            
            // Load all values (vectorizable memory access)
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                temp_vals[j] = aa[j][i];
            }
            
            // Compute prefix sum of mask using sequential accumulation
            // This is the only non-vectorizable part due to true dependency
            int prefix_sum[256];
            int count = 0;
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                prefix_sum[j] = count;
                count += mask[j];
            }
            
            // Final write loop: use precomputed offsets (vectorizable scatter)
            // Remove branching by using mask as write condition
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                if (mask[j]) {
                    flat_2d_array[k + 1 + prefix_sum[j]] = temp_vals[j];
                }
            }
            
            // Update global counter
            k += count;
        }
    }
}

```

UNIT TEST PASS

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

If the access pattern involves using a[i-1] and updating a[i] within the same iteration, a dependency exists. Attempt to reorder the instructions to resolve this dependency.

## FEEDBACK OUTPUT
## Analysis  

### 1. Compilation Errors in Optimized Code  
The optimized code compiles without errors (no syntax errors reported).  

---

### 2. Semantic Comparison with Simple Test Cases  

The original code iterates over `bb[j][i]` (column-major in the inner loop for `bb`), and when `bb[j][i] > 0`, it stores `aa[j][i]` into `flat_2d_array` sequentially across all `i` and `j`.  

The optimized code processes one column `i` at a time:  
- Computes a mask `mask[j]` for `bb[j][i] > 0`.  
- Loads `aa[j][i]` into `temp_vals[j]`.  
- Scatters `temp_vals[j]` into `flat_2d_array[k+1+count]` where `count` is a running prefix sum of `mask` for that column.  

**Potential issue**:  
In the original code, `k` is global across all columns, and the order of storage is **row-major within each column** (since `j` changes fastest for fixed `i`).  
In the optimized code, the order is preserved because for each `i`, it iterates `j` from 0 to 255 and appends qualifying elements in the same order.  
Thus, the semantics are identical.  

Unit test passed, so semantics are correct.  

---

### 3. Vectorization Analysis (Optimized Code Only)  

Compiler feedback shows:  
- Loop at line 19 (mask computation) is interleaved but not vectorized because “cost-model indicates vectorization is not beneficial.”  
- Loop at line 24 (loading `temp_vals`) same as above.  
- Loop at line 31 (prefix sum and scatter) **not vectorized** due to a value used outside the loop (true dependency on `count` and scatter address depending on `count`).  

**Why vectorization fails**:  
The scatter part (`flat_2d_array[k+1+count] = temp_vals[j]`) has a **loop-carried dependency** on `count` (true dependency). This prevents auto-vectorization because each iteration’s store address depends on the previous iteration’s `count` value.  

**Optimization opportunity**:  
The mask computation and value loading loops are already vectorizable in principle, but the compiler’s cost model decided not to vectorize them (possibly due to small trip count 256).  
The main bottleneck is the prefix-sum scatter loop, which cannot be auto-vectorized as written.  

To improve:  
We could use **vectorized prefix sum (SIMD scan)** and **vectorized gather/scatter** if targeting AVX-512, but that requires manual intrinsics. For automatic vectorization, we must eliminate the loop-carried dependency in the scatter.  
One approach: compute prefix sum of mask first (vectorizable using SIMD scan), then scatter using indexed stores (still not auto-vectorizable in C without intrinsics).  

Since the current code still has a non-vectorized loop due to true dependency, and there is an optimization opportunity (the mask and load loops could be fused and vectorized with larger trip counts), the code is **not fully optimized**.  

---

**Conclusion**:  
- Semantics: correct.  
- Vectorization: not fully achieved (scatter loop not vectorized).  
- Optimization opportunity exists → **FAIL**.

            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                if (mask[j]) {
                    flat_2d_array[k + 1 + count] = temp_vals[j];
                    count++;
                }
            }
            
            // Update global counter
            k += count;
        }
    }
}

### 2. Semantic Comparison with Simple Test Cases  

The original code iterates over `bb[j][i]` (column-major in the inner loop for `bb`), and when `bb[j][i] > 0`, it stores `aa[j][i]` into `flat_2d_array` sequentially across all `i` and `j`.  

The optimized code processes one column `i` at a time:  
- Computes a mask `mask[j]` for `bb[j][i] > 0`.  
- Loads `aa[j][i]` into `temp_vals[j]`.  
- Scatters `temp_vals[j]` into `flat_2d_array[k+1+count]` where `count` is a running prefix sum of `mask` for that column.  

**Potential issue**:  
In the original code, `k` is global across all columns, and the order of storage is **row-major within each column** (since `j` changes fastest for fixed `i`).  
In the optimized code, the order is preserved because for each `i`, it iterates `j` from 0 to 255 and appends qualifying elements in the same order.  
Thus, the semantics are identical.  

Unit test passed, so semantics are correct.  

---

### 3. Vectorization Analysis (Optimized Code Only)  

Compiler feedback shows:  
- Loop at line 19 (mask computation) is interleaved but not vectorized because “cost-model indicates vectorization is not beneficial.”  
- Loop at line 24 (loading `temp_vals`) same as above.  
- Loop at line 31 (prefix sum and scatter) **not vectorized** due to a value used outside the loop (true dependency on `count` and scatter address depending on `count`).  

**Why vectorization fails**:  
The scatter part (`flat_2d_array[k+1+count] = temp_vals[j]`) has a **loop-carried dependency** on `count` (true dependency). This prevents auto-vectorization because each iteration’s store address depends on the previous iteration’s `count` value.  

**Optimization opportunity**:  
The mask computation and value loading loops are already vectorizable in principle, but the compiler’s cost model decided not to vectorize them (possibly due to small trip count 256).  
The main bottleneck is the prefix-sum scatter loop, which cannot be auto-vectorized as written.  

To improve:  
We could use **vectorized prefix sum (SIMD scan)** and **vectorized gather/scatter** if targeting AVX-512, but that requires manual intrinsics. For automatic vectorization, we must eliminate the loop-carried dependency in the scatter.  
One approach: compute prefix sum of mask first (vectorizable using SIMD scan), then scatter using indexed stores (still not auto-vectorizable in C without intrinsics).  

Since the current code still has a non-vectorized loop due to true dependency, and there is an optimization opportunity (the mask and load loops could be fused and vectorized with larger trip counts), the code is **not fully optimized**.  

---

**Conclusion**:  
- Semantics: correct.  
- Vectorization: not fully achieved (scatter loop not vectorized).  
- Optimization opportunity exists → **FAIL**.

Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops

void s343_opt(int iterations, float *flat_2d_array, float aa[256][256], float bb[256][256]) {
    for (int nl = 0; nl < 10 * (iterations / 256); nl++) {
        int k = -1;
        
        // Process each column independently
        for (int i = 0; i < 256; i++) {
            // Temporary arrays for this column
            float temp_vals[256];
            unsigned char mask[256];
            int indices[256];
            
            // Loop distribution: separate mask computation and value loading
            // First compute all masks (vectorizable comparison)
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                mask[j] = (bb[j][i] > (float)0.);
            }
            
            // Load all values (vectorizable memory access)
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                temp_vals[j] = aa[j][i];
            }
            
            // Compute prefix sum of mask and store indices
            int count = 0;
            for (int j = 0; j < 256; j++) {
                if (mask[j]) {
                    indices[count] = j;
                    count++;
                }
            }
            
            // Scatter values using computed indices (vectorizable memory store)
            for (int j = 0; j < count; j++) {
                flat_2d_array[k + 1 + j] = temp_vals[indices[j]];
            }
            
            // Update global counter
            k += count;
        }
    }
}

## FEEDBACK OUTPUT
## Analysis

### 1. Compilation Check
The optimized code compiles successfully (no compilation errors). The compiler feedback shows only remarks about vectorization decisions, not errors.

### 2. Semantic Comparison
The optimized code has **incorrect semantics**. Let's analyze with a simple example:

**Test Case:**
- `iterations = 256` → `10*(iterations/256) = 10`
- `bb[0][0] = 1.0` (positive), `bb[1][0] = -1.0` (negative)
- `aa[0][0] = 100.0`, `aa[1][0] = 200.0`

**Original code execution:**
- `k = -1`
- Process column i=0:
  - j=0: `bb[0][0] > 0` → true, `k=0`, `flat_2d_array[0] = aa[0][0] = 100.0`
  - j=1: `bb[1][0] > 0` → false, skip
- Continue with other columns...

**Optimized code execution:**
- `k = -1`
- Process column i=0:
  - Compute mask: `mask[0]=1`, `mask[1]=0`
  - Load values: `temp_vals[0]=100.0`, `temp_vals[1]=200.0`
  - Compute indices: `indices[0]=0`, `count=1`
  - Scatter: `flat_2d_array[k+1+0] = flat_2d_array[0] = temp_vals[indices[0]] = temp_vals[0] = 100.0`
  - Update k: `k = -1 + 1 = 0`

**The problem:** The optimized code processes each column independently, but the original code uses a **global** `k` counter that accumulates across all columns. The optimized code's approach of processing columns independently with local counting and then updating `k` is actually correct for this aspect.

However, there's a **more serious issue**: The optimized code processes columns in the wrong order relative to the original code's memory layout.

**Memory layout analysis:**
- Original: `aa[j][i]` and `bb[j][i]` where `j` is row (inner loop), `i` is column (outer loop)
- Original accesses: Column-major order (i outer, j inner)
- Optimized: Processes column i, then within column processes all rows j
- This matches the original access pattern

Actually, the optimized code appears semantically correct. The compiler feedback shows "UNIT TEST PASS", confirming this.

### 3. Vectorization Analysis
The compiler feedback indicates:
1. All three inner loops are marked as "interleaved loop (interleaved count: 4)" - this means they were vectorized!
2. However, they also have remarks "the cost-model indicates that vectorization is not beneficial" - this suggests the compiler vectorized them but thinks it might not be worth it.
3. The loop at line 31 has "loop not vectorized: value that could not be identified as reduction is used outside the loop" - this is the prefix sum computation.

**Key issues:**
1. **The prefix sum loop (line 31)** cannot be vectorized due to the dependency on `count` (carried dependency).
2. **The scatter loop (line 39)** has variable trip count `count`, making vectorization difficult.
3. The compiler vectorized the comparison and load loops but with reservations about benefit.

**Optimization opportunities:**
1. **Eliminate the prefix sum dependency** by using vectorized population count.
2. **Improve memory access patterns** - the current column-major access is suboptimal.
3. **Consider SIMD-friendly conditional store** instead of gather-scatter.

### Conclusion
The optimized code is **semantically correct** but has **vectorization issues**. The prefix sum computation creates a loop-carried dependency that prevents vectorization. The scatter operation with variable indices also hinders vectorization.

**FAIL** - There are optimization opportunities to improve vectorization:
1. Use vectorized mask processing with `_mm256_movemask_ps` and population count
2. Consider transposing data for better cache locality
3. Use SIMD gather instructions if available
4. Restructure to use conditional store instead of gather-scatter

The code is not "fully optimized" as required by criterion 4, since further modifications could enable better vectorization of the prefix sum and scatter operations.
